<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, February 8, 2022
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, February 8, 2022, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, 100 East Code Talkers Drive, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to
meet in a closed executive session to discuss certain matters and for legal advice on any item as indicated on the following
agenda, pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3). 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors finds that it is in the best interest of the Community to conduct this public meeting using
a hybrid method in order to serve the public interest. Board members will participate in the meeting, in-person in the Board
of Supervisors Chambers in Holbrook, or via Zoom. Limited seating will be available for the public.
To Join Zoom Meeting now, click here: 
https://us06web.zoom.us/j/84264615494?pwd=a3ZVWS9YWUpSV1BOWDRvUVVNbHFyZz09
Web Conference:
Call In Number: 1-346-248-7799 or 1-669-900-6833 (you may use either teleconference number)
Meeting ID: 842 6461 5494
Passcode: 945459
If you would like to speak during Call to the Public please take the following actions: 
1. Email Melissa Buckley at, melissa.buckley@navajocountyaz.gov
2. Identify the Board of Supervisors' meeting date, the agenda item # and title.
3. Name & Telephone Number
4. Comments or questions should be sent by 5:00 pm. the day prior to the Board meeting.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting, so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, except items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo County
website, www.navajocountyaz.gov
“NOTICE TO PARENTS AND LEGAL GUARDIANS: Parents and legal guardians have the right to consent before Navajo County makes a video or
voice recording of a minor child, pursuant to A.R.S. § 1-602(A)(9). The Navajo County Board of Supervisors regular meetings are recorded and may
be viewed on Navajo County’s website, and social media pages. If you permit your child to attend/participate in a televised Navajo County Board of
Supervisors meeting, a recording will be made, and your child’s picture may be posted on Navajo County’s social media pages. You may exercise
your right not to consent by not allowing your child to attend/participate in the meeting.” 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to 5
minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature and
will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be removed
from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes:  January 25, 2022  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000, January 2022  
 
c. Sympathy Letters to Melissa Buckley (Board of Supervisors), Sadie Lister (RAC)

<!-- page 2 -->
d. Conduct a board member election for White Mountain Lake Special Road Dist.#2 and White
Mountain Summer Home DWID on May 17, 2022
 
 
e. Amendment Number 3 to the Slurry Seal Contract #B19-01-007 extending the contract one (1)
additional year and increasing the rate for the Winslow area to 1.45 per square yard and the
Show Low, Pinetop-Lakeside area to 1.35 per square yard
 
 
f. Extension of Mr. Wendell DeCross's term on the Planning and Zoning Commission until June
23, 2022, or until his replacement is appointed prior to that date
 
 
g. Sole source agreement with Mutualink for upcoming school safety interoperability project  
 
3. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
1. Amendment Number 1 to the Intergovernmental Agreement, Contract number
IGA2021-038 changing to CTR057323, allowing for line items to be moved to better meet
resource needs in responding to COVID
 
 
2. Annual renewal of the Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County and Coconino
County Public Health Services for Medical Examiner services
 
 
3. Memorandum of Understanding between the Navajo County Sheriff's Office (NCSO) and the
Navajo County Public Health Services District (NCPHSD) for records management and
dispatch services
 
 
b. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. Consideration of the acceptance of the FY2021 Emergency Management Performance Grant.  
Presented By: Catrina Jenkins
 
4. BOARD OF EQUALIZATION:  
 
a. Discussion and action regarding Petition for Review of Taxpayer Notice of Claim APN
409-22-027; Swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners. 
 
Presented By: Melissa Buckley/Cammy Darris
 
5. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Presentation of TRACKS Annual Report  
Presented By: Lynn Krigbaum, Vice President of TRACKS

<!-- page 3 -->
b. Consideration of a Liquor License for Amy S. Nations at Al & Diane's Red Onion Lounge
located at 1931 Highway 260, Overgaard, AZ.
 
Presented By: Leah Thomas
 
6. ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
 
a. Consideration of the development of an 18-hole disc golf course on County property
surrounding the Holbrook County Complex to complement and enhance the recreational
opportunities provided to the public. 
 
Presented By: Jared Robinson/Torel Nichols
 
7. COOPERATIVE EXTENSION:  
 
a. Presentation of Cooperative Extension Programs and Ongoing Events  
Presented By: Mike Hauser, Director
 
8. LIBRARY DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
a. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. Presentation of Navajo County Library District's Annual Report  
Presented By: David Ehrensperger
 
9. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
a. Contract signed by County Manager/Assistant County Manager pursuant to Board of
Supervisors Resolution: Amendment Number 5, Contract for Professional Services with the
Tomhave Group Inc., to "Advocate for the Little Colorado at Winslow Levee, advance
progress of surface transportation on American Indian reservations located in Navajo County,
and advance public health policies of residents at disproportionate risk of contracting
COVID-19 and other life-threatening diseases”
 
 
b. PLANNING & ZONING:  
 
1. Consideration and adoption of Resolution 02-2022 approving/denying a special use permit
that allows construction of an RV and boat storage facility on 4.64 acres, located north of
State Route 260 and west of Burton Road, in the Linden area. APN# 409-22-007S, T10N,
R21E, S06 of the GSRM.
 
Presented By: Berrin Nejad
 
10. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and

<!-- page 4 -->
10. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and
Board members.
 
 
 
11. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: February 4, 2022 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 9:45 a.m. By: MWB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Title: COE for May 17, 2022 Election
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director
Department: Elections

Mountain Summer Home DWID on May 17, 2022

White Mountain Summer Home DWID to conduct election for 2 Board Members on May 17,

<!-- page 420 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 2. e.        
Meeting Date: 02/08/2022  
Title: Approval of Amendment No. 3 to Slurry Seal Contract #B19-01-007
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Stevie Studer, Account Specialist
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Amendment Number 3 to the Slurry Seal Contract #B19-01-007 extending the contract one (1)
additional year and increasing the rate for the Winslow area to 1.45 per square yard and the Show
Low, Pinetop-Lakeside area to 1.35 per square yard
Background:
Navajo County Public Works maintains approximately 730 miles of roads throughout the County,
of which approximately 330 miles are paved.  Application of a slurry seal on asphalt roads
extends the life of the pavement.  On February 26, 2019, the Board of Supervisors approved the
contract with American Pavement Preservation, LLC for Slurry Seal Contract #B19-01-007. This
contract has worked very well for Navajo County and upon written mutual agreement, Navajo
County may renew the contract for up to four (4) additional one-year terms. This would be the
third renewal and concurrence has been received by the contractor with an increase of
approximately 3% per square yard. This contract does allow for an increase in the event economic
conditions are such that price increases become necessary.  The vendor has submitted to the
Public Works Department a formal request for the increase and has provided supporting
documentation. The increase is still lower than the second-lowest bid received in 2019.  Staff has
reviewed the request and recommends approval.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2022&2023
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
Funds are budgeted under capital outlay for FY 2022 & 2023.
Attachments
American Pavement Preservation Concurrence 
Navajo County Concurrence 
Contract Amendment No. 3 
2022 Rates

<!-- page 421 -->
Form Review
Inbox Reviewed By Date
Jeanine Carruthers Jeanine Carruthers 02/01/2022 08:38 AM
Brandt Clark Brandt Clark 02/01/2022 10:12 AM
Public Works Director John Osgood 02/01/2022 12:10 PM
Form Started By: Stevie Studer Started On: 01/31/2022 04:02 PM
Final Approval Date: 02/01/2022

<!-- page 422 -->
1
Jeanine Carruthers
From: Eric Reimschiissel <ericr@americanpave.com>
Sent: Tuesday, January 11, 2022 4:25 PM
To: Jeanine Carruthers
Cc: Stevie Studer
Subject: RE: Slurry Seal Contract
Attachments: Navajo County renewal proposed revised unit prices  1-11-22.pdf
Caution: This email originated from outside of Navajo County. 
Hi Jeanine and Steve 
 
In follow up to the renewal for the Slurry Seal Contract,  American Pavement Preservation wants to renew the 
current contract.  I feel we have a great working relationship and because of the past experience, both of us are 
able to make the project go smooth and un
‐eventful.   
 
During the last year due to the cost of crude oil,  the cost of asphalt has risen more than anyone ever 
expected.   We all see and hear about the frustrations with the high cost of gasoline and diesel fuel which effects 
our daily lives, which from an asphalt contractor 
is the same frustration for asphalt. 
 
The existing contract was wisely written with an Arizona DOT index, based on the bid date listed asphalt cost 
amount to allow adjustments for the movement of the price of the asphalt either increasing or decreasing.  The 
initial ADOT index price at bid date was
 $469.00 and as of today the current ADOT index price is $508.00.  This 
provides for a $39.00 increase in the asphalt price.  The price has increased 8.3 percent.  The projections for the 
price of crude are to continue to rise through the spring and summer of the 2022 construction season which is not 
helpful in any sense.   
 
There is some good news as I have a long term relationship with our supplier, Ergon Asphalt and Emulsions and 
we have negotiated a number less than the $39.00.   I have stressed to them the need for us to be able to renew 
this
 slurry seal project with Navajo County.  The increase would be less than the 8.3 percent as we are asking for a 
0.029 percent adjustment in the unit price of the slurry seal items only to reflect only the asphalt cost increase.  I 
am only asking for an increase due to asphalt, we will accept the other price increases in fuel, all other materials 
and the project labor.  Please see the attached spread sheet indicating the amount of the price adjustment we are 
proposing and hope that this is acceptable.  Our supplier needs to get commitments from us to insure the asphalt 
price does not increase further.  
 
Please review the attached spread sheet with the unit prices listed and let me know if this is acceptable.  I am 
hoping this meets your expectations.   After your review please feel free to contact me with any questions. 
 
Thank you again.  We are all looking
 forward to another successful slurry seal season working together. 
 
Eric

<william.flake@navajocountyaz.gov> 
Subject: FW: Slurry Seal Contract 
 
American Pavement Preservation is interested in renewing the Slurry Seal Contract an additional year.  This would be the 3
rd 
extension out of the allowable 5.  They are requesting a slight increase, see attached proposal from American Pavement 
Preservation.  I’ve also included the original Bid Tab and it looks like they are requesting a slight increase with the renewal, 
which is still less than the other submittals received a few years back.  The contract does allow for an increase (see below). 
 
It is the intention of Navajo County that prices quoted shall remain firm during the term of this contract.  In 
the event economic conditions are such that price increases or decreases become necessary, the vendo r
must submit to the Public Works Department a formal request for increases with supporting documents, i.e., 
manufacturers' price lists, suppliers' notices of price increases, etc.  Navajo County will review such requests
and test the market to determine whether or not the prices are reasonable.  Navajo County shall use the
ADOT publications “Price Adjustment for Bituminous Material” when considering escalation increases during
the term of the contract.  Navajo County may, at its sole discretion, approve or disapprove the
increase/decrease or discontinue procurement of specified items of the bid, and proceed to obtain the item(s) 
on the open market.  Navajo County will not consider any price increases during the first one hundred fifty
(150) days of this contract. Price increases will not be honored and will not be effective until approval by the
Board of Supervisors, and a formal letter of approval is issued to the vendor.  Price increases will not be
honored for any “Work Orders” that are open during the time of request.  For purposes of this contract, the
base price is the ADOT January 30, 2019 schedule. 
 
Please let us know if you’d like us to proceed with the renewal and increase. 
 
Thank you.

Hi Jeanine and Steve 
 
In follow up to the renewal for the Slurry Seal Contract,  American Pavement Preservation wants to renew the 
current contract.  I feel we have a great working relationship and because of the past experience, both of us are 
able to make the project go smooth and un
‐eventful.   
 
During the last year due to the cost of crude oil,  the cost of asphalt has risen more than anyone ever 
expected.   We all see and hear about the frustrations with the high cost of gasoline and diesel fuel which effects 
our daily lives, which from an asphalt contractor is the same frustration for asphalt. 
 
The existing contract was wisely written with an Arizona DOT index, based on the bid date listed asphalt cost 
amount to allow adjustments for the movement of the price of the asphalt either increasing or decreasing.  The 
initial ADOT index price at bid date 
was $469.00 and as of today the current ADOT index price is $508.00.  This 
provides for a $39.00 increase in the asphalt price.  The price has increased 8.3 percent.  The projections for the 
price of crude are to continue to rise through the spring and summer of the 2022 construction season which is not 
helpful in any sense.   
 
There is some good news as I have a long term relationship with our supplier, Ergon Asphalt and Emulsions and 
we have negotiated a number less than the $39.00.   I have stressed to them the need for us to be able to renew 
this slurry seal project with Navajo County.  The increase would be less than the 8.3 percent as we are asking for a 
0.029 percent adjustment in the unit price of the slurry seal items only to reflect only the asphalt cost increase.  I 
am only asking for an increase due to asphalt, we will accept the other price increases in fuel, all other materials 
and the project labor.  Please see the attached spread sheet indicating the amount of the price adjustment we are 
proposing and hope that this is acceptable.  Our supplier needs to get commitments from us to insure the asphalt 
price does not increase further.  
 
Please review the attached spread sheet with the unit prices listed and let me know if this is acceptable.  I am 
hoping this meets your expectations.   After your review please feel free to contact me with any questions. 
 
Thank you again.  We are all 
looking forward to another successful slurry seal season working together. 
 
Eric

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. f.        
Meeting Date: 02/08/2022  
Title: Extend the Term of Planning and Zoning Commissioner Mr. Wendell DeCross -
District V
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Extension of Mr. Wendell DeCross's term on the Planning and Zoning Commission until June 23,
2022, or until his replacement is appointed prior to that date
Background:
Mr. DeCross has indicated his intention to resign from the Planning and Zoning Commission after
years of service.  His current term expired on January 31, 2022, but he has expressed willingness
to stay on the Commission until June 23, 2022 or until his replacement is appointed prior to that
date.  Chairwoman Whitesinger has been consulted, approves the extension of Mr. DeCross's
appointment as described, and plans to recognize him more formally at a future Board of
Supervisors meeting.

Title: IGA CTR057323 ELC Grant COVID-19 Community Testing

Motion before the Board:
Amendment Number 1 to the Intergovernmental Agreement, Contract number
IGA2021-038 changing to CTR057323, allowing for line items to be moved to better meet
resource needs in responding to COVID
Background:
Changes to amendment highlighted in red.  Contract number changing to CTR057323. 
Amendment allows for ability to move originally awarded dollars among line items to better meet
resource needs in responding to Covid.  Contract number also amended cost reimbursement with
funding period of May 18, 2020 through November 30, 2022
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 22
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
Provides ability to move funding among line items.  IGA is cost reimbursement.  No change to
original contract award amount.  Contract runs through 11-30-2022 and is designated to be utilized
towards specific allowable staffing and other resources to continue covid surveillance and enhance
community testing availability.

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. a. 2.        
Meeting Date: 02/08/2022  
Title: Update IGA with Coconino County 
Submitted For: Janelle Linn, Health Director 
Submitted By: Rhonda Krouse, Code Enforcement Division Manager 
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
Annual renewal of the Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County and Coconino County Public
Health Services for Medical Examiner services
Background:
The Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County and Coconino County Public Health Services is
being considered for renewal for another year.  There have been no changes or updates to the proposed IGA. 
The Coconino Public Health Services provides Navajo County with post-mortem examinations as well as
other medical documentation on decedents of Navajo County.  Recommend approval.

Title: Memorandum of Understanding between NCSO and NCPHD

Memorandum of Understanding between the Navajo County Sheriff's Office (NCSO) and the Navajo
County Public Health Services District (NCPHSD) for records management and dispatch services
Background:
The Navajo County Public Health Services District is seeking the use of the NSCO record management
system (Spillman Technologies), as well as their dispatch and radio communication services.  These
valuable assets will allow the animal care and control officers a secure data storage program for incidents
related to animal care and control.  It will also provide officer safety through the use of dispatch
communications to know their specific location if law enforcement assistance is requested. Recommend
approval.

Memorandum of Understanding 
Navajo County Sheriff’s Office, and Navajo County Public Health District  
 
This Memorandum of Understanding (“MOU”) is created by and between the Navajo County Sheriff’s 
Office (hereinafter “NCSO”),, and Navajo County Public Health District (hereinafter “NCPHD”),, also 
referred to collectively as the “Parties.”   
 
WHEREAS: 
A.  The Navajo County Public Health District is seeking to obtain a database system to house 
their animal control department’s record management system , and;   
B. The Navajo County Public Health District is seeking to use dispatch and radio communication 
services, and;  
C. The Navajo County Sheriff’s Office is an existing customer of the Motorola Flex/ Spillman 
Technologies, Inc., and; 
D. The Navajo County Sheriff’s Office can adequately assist NCPHD with managing their record 
management system through their contractual relationship with Motorola Flex/ Spillman 
Technologies, Inc ., and; 
E. The Navajo County Sheriff’s Office can adequately assist NCPHD with the use of dispatch 
and radio communication services.  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the Parties hereto, in consideration of the covenants hereinafter set forth, agree as 
follows: 
 
1.   PURPOSE. 
The purpose of this MOU is to clarify and define the roles, functions, and 
responsibilities between NCSO and NCPHD to facilitate the use of dispatch and radio 
communications and the use of Spillman Records Management Systems . This MOU is not a 
contract.  Nor is this MOU drafted or intended to be a contract or used as a substitute for a 
contract.  Instead, this MOU serves to state the parties’ intended actions and facilitate the 
actions and functions stated and discussed below.
 
 
2.   OBLIGATIONS 
A. SCOPE OF SPILLMAN RMS AND MDC SERVICES:  
1. RESPONSIBILITIES OF NCSO: The Navajo County Sheriff’s Office shall provide 
Spillman Records Management Systems (RMS) and Mobile Data Computer (MDC) 
services for the NCPHD including dispatching NCPHD members to calls for service, 
computerized record checks and statistical recordkeeping of such calls for service. This 
service shall be provided as necessary for the operational requirements of NCPHD and 
shall be subject to the following terms and conditions: 
a. NCSO shall manage and maintain NCPHD radio logs, computer aided 
dispatch, and mobile data computer system, all of which are within Spillman 
software. 
b. NCSO shall enter into NCSO’s Spillman RMS, any and all day to day radio 
and data records logged and performed by NCPHD members. 
c. NCSO shall act as Spillman system administrator for NCPHD in accordance 
with industry standards and to the satisfaction of NCPHD. 
d. NCSO shall coordinate any Spillman software upgrades with NCPHD.

<!-- page 469 -->
2 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
e. NCSO shall be NCPHD point of contact for any requests for changes, 
maintenance, or modifications to the Spillman RMS software. 
f. NCSO does not authorize NCPHD the ability to create, maintain or modify 
NCPHD user accounts within the Spillman R MS. NCSO only authorizes 
NCPHD the day to day use of Spillman for report and record management 
purposes.   
g. NCSO hereby agrees that current and future NCPHD employees shall be 
designated as authorized users of NCSO Spillman RMS and MDC systems. 
h. NCSO agrees to provide sufficient training resources to ensure that NCPHD 
members are fully trained in the operation of the Spillman RMS and MDC 
systems. 
i. NCSO shall provide necessary testing and application processing for any 
Spillman MDC users from NCPHD that will be accessing ACJIS 
information.  
j. NCSO shall immediately notify NCPHD of any abuse, misuse, or erroneous 
practice of any NCPHD member within the Spillman RMS or MDC systems. 
k. NCSO reserves the right to block or terminate use of the Spillman systems 
to any NCPHD u ser for any violation or misuse of the Spillman systems 
and/or NCIC and/or ACJIS information.     
 
2. RESPONSIBILITIES OF NCPHD: 
a. NCPHD shall conform to the rules, regulations, procedures and policies of 
NCSO regarding use of NCSO’s voice radio communications and dispatch 
systems and the requirements of the Arizona Criminal Justice Information 
System. Copies of all NCSO rules, regulations, procedures and policies 
regarding use of NCSO dispatch and all ACJIS queries are attached as 
“Exhibit A”. NCPHD will be promptly notified of any revisions or additions 
to those rules, regulations, procedures and policies.  
b. NCPHD will request NCSO with at least 7 days’ notice for any new user to 
be created and will provide notice with as much reasonable time as necessary 
for any maintenance or modifications of current users.  
c. NCPHD shall notify NCSO immediately, without delay, o f any member 
violating any ACJIS or NCSO procedure or policy  regarding the Spillman 
systems or use of ACJIS information.  
d. NCPHD shall notify NCSO immedia tely, without delay, of any user’s 
termination or loss of employment with NCPHD.  
e. NCPHD shall maintain its MDC devices to minimum operational and 
functional standards. MDC devices require minimum safety security 
functions at all times per NCIC and ACJIS re gulations and NCSO policy 
and procedure.   
f. NCPHD shall maintain Terminal Operator Certifications (TOC) at all times 
for all users of the Spillman RMS and MDC systems. TOC recertifications 
occur every two years through NCSO Dispatch. Inability to maintain this 
certification will result in the NCPHD user being blocked from using the 
Spillman systems.

<!-- page 470 -->
3 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
B. SCOPE OF DISPATCH AND RADIO COMMUNICATION SERVICES: 
1. RESPONSIBILITIES OF NCSO:  NCSO shall provide dispatch and radio services 
for NCPHD, including dispatching NCPHD members to calls for service, 
computerized record checks and statistical record keeping of such calls for service. 
This service shall be provided 24 hours per day, 7 days per week with the following 
terms and conditions:  
a. NCSO shall answer and triage all incoming telephone calls for service for 
NCPHD and forward those calls to the appropriate NCPHD member(s). 
b. NCSO shall enter into NCSO’s Spillman RMS and into ACJIS, any a nd 
all day to day radio and data records requested by NCPHD members. 
c. NCSO shall maintain the communications infrastructure necessary to 
allow for NCPHD voice radio communications and operations. 
d. NCSO agrees to provide sufficient training resources to ensur e that 
NCPHD members are fully trained in the operation and use of the radio 
systems. 
e. NCSO will provide NCPHD with a copy of NCSO policy and procedure 
“Exhibit A” regarding the use of the radio and dispatch communications 
center.  
f. NCSO agrees to  provide re sources to  assist with any  equipment 
installation or  malfunction regarding the radio or communications 
equipment used by the NCPHD.  
g. NCSO agrees to notify NCPHD in advance of any infrastructure or 
software changes that affects the ability of NCPHD to communicate with 
the NCSO dispatch center. 
h. NCSO shall immediately notify NCPHD of any abuse, misuse, or 
erroneous practice of any NCPHD user regarding the use of the radio.  
i. NCSO reserves the right to block or terminate any NCPHD user from 
using the communications radio channels for any violations or misuse of 
the radio.   
 
2. RESPONSIBILITIES OF NCPHD:  
a. NCPHD users shall adhere to NCSO policy and procedure regarding the use 
and communications of NCSO dispatch and radio communications services.  
b. NCPHD shall notify NCSO immediately, without delay, o f any member 
violating any NCSO radio and communications policy or procedure.   
c. It is the responsibility of NCPHD to purchase and acquire their own 
communications equipment. (Per this agreement 2.B.1.f – NCSO will assist 
as requested with install and repair of any radio communications equipment, 
but the primary responsibility rests with NCPHD).  
d. NCPHD shall maintain its mobile and portable radios to minimum 
operational and functional standards. 
e. NCPHD shall provide reasonable notice to NCSO of any other needs or 
requests regarding the use of NCSO dispatch and communication services.

<!-- page 471 -->
4 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
C. RECORDS RETENTION AND REQUESTS: 
1. The Navajo County Sheriff’s Office will be the host agency for all records created 
and stored within the Spillman RMS database for NCPHD. However, NCSO will not 
be the default custodian of records regarding requests and dissemination of any 
record pertaining to NCPHD. Any public record request received by NCSO 
pertaining to any record related to NCPHD will be forwarded to NCPHD to complete 
the request and track all records disseminated.  
2. NCPHD shall retain and disseminate their own records within the Spillman RMS 
database according to Arizona Revised Statutes and the Arizona State Library, 
Archives and Public Records Schedules.  
3. NCPHD will be responsible for redacting any and all secure and confidential 
information within any disseminated record. NCSO will not be held liable for any 
information disseminated by NCPHD.  
 
 
3.  EFFECTIVE DATE AND DURATION. This MOU shall become effective on the       1st        day 
of     February  , 2022 and shall remain in effect for five (5) years, unless either Party provides thirty 
(30) days advance written notice of termination to the other Party as set forth in Section 4 below.  Either 
Party to this MOU may request a renewal of this MOU, for additional five (5) year terms by the 
execution of a mutually agreed upon and signed Amendment to this MOU. 
 
4. TERMINATION.  To the extent permitted by law, either party may cancel this MOU at any time, 
with or without cause, upon thirty  (30) days prior written notice to the other party. In the event of 
termination or cancellation of this MOU.   
5.   COMPLIANCE WITH ALL LAWS Each Party shall comply with all federal, state, and local 
laws, rules, and regulations. 
 
6.  EXECUTION PROCEDURE  This Agreement will be executed in counterparts by t he 
governing body of each Party. 
 
7.    NON-DISCRIMINATION  The Parties will not discriminate on the grounds of age, race, color, 
national origin, religion, sex, disability, familial status, genetic information, political affiliation or any 
other protected basis in the selection, treatment and retention of employees, subcontractors or in the 
procurement of materials and leases of equipment.  The Parties also will not participate, either directly 
or indirectly, in any type of discrimination prohibited by any federal, state, or local law, including but 
not limited to Titles VI and VII of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, Section 504 of the Rehabilitation Act 
of 1973, the Age Discrimination Act of 1975, and the Genetic Information Nondiscrimination Act of 
2008. 
 
8.    LEGAL ARIZONA WORKERS ACT COMPLIANCE Parties are required to comply with 
A.R.S. § 41-4401, and hereby warrants that they will, at all times during the terms of this Agreement, 
comply with all federal immigration laws applicable to the employment of their respective employees, 
the requirements of A.R.S.    § 41-4401, and with the e-verification requirements of A.R.S. § 23-214 
(A) (together the “state and federal immigration laws”).  Parties further agree to ensure that each 
subcontractor that pe rforms any work under this Agreement likewise complies with the state and 
federal immigration laws.

Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
A breach of a warranty regarding compliance with state and federal immigration laws shall be deemed 
a material breach of the Agreement and the Party who breaches may be subject to penalties up to and 
including termination of this Agreement. 
 
Each Party retains the legal right to inspect the papers of any contractor or subcontractor employee 
working under the terms of the Agreement to ensure that the other Pa rty is complying with the 
warranties regarding compliance with the state and federal immigration laws. 
 
9. NON-APPROPRIATION This Agreement shall be subject to available funding for each Party, 
and nothing in this Agreement shall bind any Party to expenditures in excess of funds appropriated 
and allotted for the purposes outlined in this Agreement. 
 
10. NO THIRD PARTY BENEFICIARIES The Parties acknowledge and agree that the terms, 
provisions, conditions, and obligations of this Agreement are for the sol e benefit of, may be 
enforceable solely by, the Parties, and none of the terms, provisions, conditions, and obligations 
of this Agreement are for the benefit of, or may be enforced by, any person or entity not a Party 
of this Agreement. 
 
11. SIGNATURES Each Pa rty represents and warrants that all necessary approvals for this 
Agreement have been obtained, and the persons who signatures appear below have the authority 
necessary to execute this Agreement on behalf of the Parties indicated. 
 
12. INDEMNIFICATION AND JOINT DEFENSE. To the maximum extent permitted by law, 
each Party (as “Indemnitor”) agrees to indemnify, defend and hold harmless the other Party, its 
officers, officials, agents, employees, or volunteers from and against any and all claims, losses, 
liability, costs or expenses (including reasonable attorney’s fees) (hereinafter collectively referred 
to as “Claims”) arising out of  actions taken in performance of this MOU to the extent that such 
Claims are caused by the acts, omissions, negligence, misconduct, or other fault of the Indemnitor, 
its officers, officials, agents, employees, or volunteers.  If a Claim or Claims by third parties 
becomes subject to this section, the governmental parties to this MOU that are the subject of the 
Claim or Claims shall expeditiously meet to agree upon a common and mutual defense pursuant 
to the subsection below, including proportionate liability and proportionate payment of litigation 
fees, expenses and damages.
 
 
The Parties, when involved in a Claim or Claims brought by a third-party, have a common interest 
in a coordinated defense in any lawsuit. In the absence of a conflict, the Parties agree to have one 
lawyer jointly represent the defendants in the lawsuit.  The Parties agree to abide by the 
Memorandum of Understanding R egarding Joint Defense (“ MOU”) between the Arizona 
Counties Insurance Pool (“ACIP”) and the Arizona Municipal Risk Retention Pool (“AMRRP”).  
Each Party acknowledges that it has received a copy of the MOU from either ACIP or AMRRP. 
 
The obligations under this section shall survive the termination of this MOU. 
 
 GENERAL 
A. Notice.  Any notice, consent or other communication (“Notice”) required or permitted under 
this MOU shall be in writing and either delivered in person, sent by facsimile transmission, 
deposited in the United States mail, return receipt requested, or deposited with any commercial 
air courier or express service addressed as follows:

Notice shall be deemed received at the time it is personally served, on the day it is sent by

as above provided.  Any time period stated in a notice shall  be computed from the time the 
notice is deemed received.  Either party may change any portion of its notice information 
provided above by notifying the other party of the change in writing as provided in this Section. 
 
Notices sent by facsimile transmission shall also be deposited in the United States mail to the 
recipient at the above address on the same day the facsimile transmission is sent.  This 
requirement for duplicate notice is not intended to change the effective date of the notice sent 
by facsimile transmission. 
 
B. Amendment. This MOU may be amended only by a written document executed by a duly 
authorized representatives of both Parties. 
C. Third Parties. This MOU is entered into for the sole and exclusive benefit of the Parties, and 
no other person shall claim any implied right, benefit or interest in this MOU. The Parties do 
not intend to create rights in or remedies to any third party as a beneficiary of this MOU or of 
any duty, obligation, or undertaking established under this MOU. 
D. Compliance with Applicable Laws. Each Party shall comply with all applicable laws, statutes, 
ordinances, executive orders, rules, regulations, standards, and codes of federal and stat e 
governments whether or not specifically referred to in this MOU. 
E. Conflict of Interest. The Parties understand and acknowledge the provisions of A.R.S. § 38-
511 relating to cancellation of contracts due to conflicts of interest shall apply to this MOU. 
F. Dispute Resolution. If a complaint, dispute or controversy arises  out of or relates to this MOU, 
and if the dispute cannot be settled through negotiation within sixty (60) days, the Parties agree 
first to try in good faith to resolve the dispute by mediation before resorting to litigation. The 
Parties shall mutually agree upon a mediator.  Each party agrees  to bear its own costs of 
mediation, and to split the mediator fee. If mediation fails, any claim or action arising out of this 
MOU shall be brought in the Navajo County Superior Court in Holbrook, Arizona. 
G. Waiver. The waiver by any Party of any breach of any term, covenant or condition of this MOU 
shall not be deemed a waiver of such term, covenant or condition or any subsequent breach of 
the same or any other term, covenant or condition of this MOU. 
H. Uncontrollable Events. No Party shall be considered to be in default in the performance of any 
obligations under this MOU (other than obligations of a Party to pay costs and expenses) if 
failure of performance is due to an uncontrollable event.  The term “uncontrollabl e event” 
means any cause beyond the control of the Party affected, including but not limited to flood,

and restraint by court order or public authority, that by exercise of due diligence and foresight

I. Entire MOU. This MOU represents the entire MOU between the Parties and supersedes all

J. Governing Law. This MOU shall be governed by and construed in accordance with the laws of

within the State of Arizona by residents of the State of Arizona.  Any action at law, suit in 
equity or judicial proceeding for the enforcement of this MOU or any provision therefore shall

K. Severability. If any part, term or provision of this MOU shall be held illegal, unenforceable or 
in conflict with any law, the validity of the remaining portions and provisions hereof shall not 
be affected. 
L. Headings. Section headings are inserted in this MOU solely for convenience and the section

of any provision of this MOU. 
M. Counterparts. This MOU may be signed in counterparts, each of which shall be deemed an 
original, but all of which together shall constitute one and the same instrument. 
N. Other Duties Imposed by L aw. Nothing in this MOU  shall be construed as relieving the

O. Compliance with Civil Rights . The Parties agree to comply with A.R.S. Title 41, Chapter 9 
(Civil Rights), Arizona  Executive Orders 75- 5 and 99 -4 and any other federal or state laws 
relating to equal opportunity and non-discrimination, including the Americans with Disabilities 
Act. 
P. Recitals Incorporated. The Parties acknowledge that the Recitals to this MOU are true, accurate

Q. Joint Venture.  It is not intended by this MOU to, and nothing contained in this MOU shall, be 
construed to, create any partnership, joint venture or employment relationship between the 
Parties or create any employer-employee relationship between the Parties’ employees. Neither 
Party shall be liable for any debts, accounts, obligation s or other liabilities whatsoever of the 
other Party, including, but without limitation, the other Party's obligation to withhold Social 
Security and income taxes for itself or any of its employees. 
 
Each Party shall act in its individual capacity and not as an agent, employee, partner, 
joint-venturer, associate, or any other representative capacity of the other party.  Each Party 
shall be solely and entirely responsible for its acts or acts of its agents and employees during 
the performance of this MOU.   This MOU shall not be construed to imply authority to 
perform any tasks, or accept any responsibility, not expressly set forth herein.  This MOU 
shall be strictly construed against the creation of a duty or responsibility unless the  intention

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. b. 1.        
Meeting Date: 02/08/2022  
Title: FY2021 Emergency Management Performance Grant (EMPG) award 
Submitted For: Catrina Jenkins, EMP Director 
Submitted By: Catrina Jenkins, EMP Director
Department: NC Public Health Services District Division: Emergency Management
Presented By: Catrina Jenkins
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of the acceptance of the FY2021 Emergency Management Performance Grant.
Background:
Navajo County Emergency Management and Preparedness continues to request annual grant
funding through the Emergency Management Performance Grant. NCEMP will utilize these funds
to foster community preparedness and resilience for all citizens, stakeholders and staff. These
grant dollars support the Ready Navajo County alert system, several plans including the
Emergency Operations Plan, Continuity of Government and Continuity of Operations Plan, as
well as the training and exercises that support response partners and volunteers throughout the
region.  
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.
Fiscal Impact

events and meetings as well as training hours.

<!-- page 493 -->
TRACKS 2021 Annual Report 
 
Arizona’s White Mountains offer outstanding 
outdoor opportunities, great beauty, and diverse 
wildlife habitats. 200+ miles of non-motorized 
trails in the Apache-Sitgreaves National Forests 
are provided through cooperation between the US 
Forest Service, AZ Game & Fish and TRACKS, a 
500+ member volunteer organization that 
maintains & builds the White Mountains Trail 
System (WMTS). Formed in the late 1980’s, 
TRACKS began as an ad-hoc committee of the 
Town of Pinetop-Lakeside and became a 501.C.3 
non-profit. The WMTS attracts hikers, equestrians, 
trail runners, mountain bikers, cross country 
skiers, bird watchers & photographers, supports 
the regional economy, and promotes healthy 
lifestyles. In the past 5 years, TRACKS distributed 
70,000+ White Mountains Trail System brochures, 
which is a strong indicator of the trail system 
popularity. 
 
In 2021, TRACKS trail crew worked 2037 hours on 
the following trails: Blue Ridge, Panorama, 
Osprey, 4 Springs, Land of Pioneers, Los Burros, 
Chipmunk, Los Caballos, Lookout Connector,

<!-- page 494 -->
Timber Mesa, and Buena Vista. 
http://independentsector.org/volunteer_time 
valued AZ volunteer work at $26.94/hr; thus, 
TRACKS trail work was valued at $54,877 as 
support to the community, US Forest Service & 
White Mountains Trail System users. 
 
TRACKS members also reported other 
volunteering of 11,124 hours, valued at $299,681, 
at events & other community service activities. In 
addition to the valuation of TRACKS total 
volunteering ($354,588), the White Mountains Trail 
System, Tour of the White Mountains and 
triathlons provide major economic benefits to the 
region, e.g. the 2021 Epic Rides 25th year of the 
TWM had 1150 registered riders, many supporters 
and 120+ volunteers, who used local businesses. 
 
TRACKS improved trail safety by installing coded 
white trail diamonds every ¼ mile on all WMTS 
trails, gps’ing the location of each diamond, 
& providing a map with gps coordinates to all 
regional emergency responders & dispatchers. 
911 callers tell the operator the code of the 
nearest diamond so that responders can locate 
them readily. Grants from AZ Game & Fish

<!-- page 495 -->
Heritage Fund, Navajo County and Hon Dah 
Resort helped cover project costs. Since 2013, all 
rescues have been 45 min. or less, with one 
responding officer. AZ State Parks Board voted to 
recommend TRACKS Emergency Response 
project as a possible model for all trails in AZ, 
& the project was featured as a “best use” of AZ 
Game & Fish Heritage Fund awards in a report to 
the AZ Legislature. 
 
Trail maps are free on 
www.trackswhitemountains.org Free 
membership includes a newsletter. Donations are 
tax deductible, the primary financial support for 
TRACKS, and are used for trail maintenance 
& organizational expenses. TRACKS is a listed 
charity on www.Smile.Amazon.com so Amazon 
users may designate it for donations. We invite 
you to join and support a wonderful group of 
dedicated outdoor enthusiasts for efforts that 
truly make a significant difference in Arizona.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. a.        
Meeting Date: 02/08/2022  
Title: Holbrook Disc Golf Course Proposal
Submitted For: Bryan Layton, Assistant County Manager 
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Administrative Services
Presented By: Jared Robinson/Torel Nichols
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of the development of an 18-hole disc golf course on County property surrounding
the Holbrook County Complex to complement and enhance the recreational opportunities
provided to the public. 
Background:
Navajo County provides several amenities on the property surrounding the Holbrook County
Complex which are open to the public and include walking trails, picnic areas, grassy areas,
racquetball courts, and basketball courts. These amenities are used by local residents, County
employees, and travelers who are looking for a place to rest and walk their dogs. The high desert
environment and buttes at the Holbrook County Complex offer unique and interesting views and
terrain. 
The high desert environment would create a one-of-a-kind disc golf course experience that
would be free-to-play and open to the public. Disc Golf is one of the fastest growing
sports in the world, takes up less than half the space of a traditional golf course, and
requires minimal investment/maintenance. A popular disc golf app would be used to
advertise the location at no cost. The disc golf app would also guide players and the course
is designed to operate without any staffing. 
 
Staff believe that the unique terrain at the County Complex is highly suited to a
one-of-a-kind recreation opportunity that is accessible and fun for players of all ages. The
proposed course has the potential to become a destination course where travelers and
players intentionally stop in Holbrook for the opportunity to play. The course layout for
the County Complex has been designed and tested by two Navajo County staff who have
extensive experience in disc golf in coordination with engineering staff from Public Works.
 
 The cost to create the disc golf course is expected to be $16,000 which includes the cost of
the metal disc golf baskets (holes) and small launch pad (concrete spaces to begin each
hole). Due to the durable nature of the materials involved, it is anticipated that
maintenance of the course will minimal. The cost of the course is proposed to be a split
partnership between the General Fund and the Health Trust Fund.

<!-- page 523 -->
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of the proposed development of a disc golf course on County property
at the Holbrook County Complex.

<!-- page 542 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY  
COOPERATIVE EXTENSION       
 ANNUAL REPORT - 2021 
This Annual Report summarizes the 2021 Navajo County Cooperative Extension programs and activities 
of 4-H Youth Development, Range and Animal Science, Family and Consumer Health Sciences,          
Supplemental Nutrition Education Programs, and Gardening/Home Horticulture.  These programs    
provided grassroots education to Navajo County residents in 2021.  In addition, 32 citizens served as              
Cooperative Extension volunteers.  These valuable volunteers enhanced Extension programming by 
assisting in delivering relevant educational programs and information to many citizens and                 
communities throughout Navajo County.  The contributions of these volunteers totaled 2,816 hours   
of service with an estimated value of $80,370. 
Cooperative Extension greatly appreciates the financial support, office space, and services provided by 
the Navajo County Board of Supervisors. Navajo County Cooperative Extension continues to leverage 
county contributions through collaborations and partnerships with many different organizations and 
groups.

<!-- page 543 -->
LIVESTOCK AND RANGE 
AGRICULTURE AND NATURAL RESOURCE 
Public Outreach and Ranch/Farm Visits 
The most popular reason for a farm/ranch visit was pasture damage and erosion issues  
           Additional farm/ranch visits were requested for: 
 Toxic Plant Identification 
 Tree/Shrub Identification 
 Tree Insect and Disease Identification 
 Forage Evaluation 
 Farm Establishment Questions 
 Wildlife Exclosure Information 
Navajo County Fair: 
Outreached to over 150 people 
Answered questions on a wide variety of 
topics from pasture management to 
native plants 
Partnered with Master Gardeners, SNAP 
and 4H to staff the booth  
Handed out over 700 pieces of infor-
mation on livestock health, invasive 
and toxic plants to livestock, livestock 
care, rangeland health and mainte-
nance, and pasture management. 
Navajo County Livestock and Range Cooperative Extension Impacts: 
 
 Education on livestock care, treatment and necessities to new and current ranchers 
and farmers helped bring a new awareness around appropriate livestock care. 
 Pasture Management outreach has helped landowners identify the correct forage 
for their specific livestock. 
 ALIRT (Arizona Livestock Incident Response Team) Toxic Plant Fact Sheets have re-
ceived over 11,000 views on the Cooperative Extension Facebook Page.

<!-- page 544 -->
HOPI 
FEDERALLY RECOGNIZED TRIBAL EXTENSION  
It has been a great year for the Hopi Federally Recognized Tribal Program (FRTEP). We have grown in 
many different directions. Many new programs that we have been working on in 2020 came to fruition 
this year. Please visit our website to see more detailed information on our programs. 
                     https://extension.arizona.edu/hopi-tribal-extension-online 
AGRICULTURAL PROGRAMMING 
 In-person Hopi Livestock Seminar that focused on vaccination protocols 
for cattle and horses, low-stress handling, and record keeping. There 
were hands-on opportunities for people to practice on live animals.  
 Partnered with the Hopi Farmers Market Committee, supporting the     
planning and implementation of this event. We also had an educational 
booth and garden demonstrations at Hopi Farmers Market held July 11, 
August 1, September 12 and October 3. 
 In Fall 2021, we supported the Apache County SNAP-Ed staff with 
erecting  a new hoop house on the Rock Point Community School campus. We have built 2  previous 
hoop houses on our current office site. 
 
 4-H & YOUTH DEVELOPMENT 
 We reached 230 K-6th grade youth at Second Mesa Day School with 4-H          
education. 
 We also reached 140 youth during an after-school program at First Mesa 
Youth Center with virtual garden sessions.  
 In Fall 2021, we taught 21 youth, K-8th grade of the Peace Academic 
Center every week, garden classes which includes sessions in the garden.  
 We’re working with Hopi Day School Parent Group to lead sessions on           
gardening and fruit trees located on their campus. 
 Hopi FRTEP Impacts  
 Developed an on-line presence for our programming. Topics include a poultry       
series, equine series, gardening workshops (topics were garden planning, Herbs, Soil 
Preparation, Potting Soil, Garden Calendar, Seed Starting, Container Gardening, and 
Passive Solar Greenhouses), Arizona Beef Quality Assurance training. 
 In January the new Hopi 4H Club was chartered and launched. We currently have 4 
members and 2 adult volunteers. We offer the Hopi Healthy Living Project and STEM 
Lego Mind Storm Coding Project.

<!-- page 545 -->
4-H 
YOUTH DEVELOPMENT 
There were 50 entries into the open class building of the    
Navajo County Fair by 12 members of 4 clubs, including four 
Cloverbuds. Exhibits were for entomology, photography, out-
door adventures, sewing poultry/embryology/egg production, 
welding, drawing, string art. FRTEP agent Susan Sekaquaptewa 
judged all the exhibits and she and program coordinator Bev 
Goodnight checked in the kids and helped put ribbons on the 
entries. There were 97 large livestock members (4-H and FFA) 
entered 9 goats, 16 lambs, 11 steers, and 77 pigs. Seven small 
stock members showed 2 cavy (guinea pigs), 9 poultry/ pigeon, 
and 6 rabbits. There were 25 horse members that participated 
in Western showmanship, gymkhana events and ranch horse 
events.  
Because of Navajo County 4-H: 
 115 market livestock members (both 4-H and FFA) learned quality assurance 
principles which taught them about the quality of the meat and food products de-
rived from their animal project. 
 84 members completed demonstrations expressing the knowledge that they 
have learned about their project during the year.

Apache and Navajo County in collaboration with Department of Education and    
Winslow Indian Health Care

<!-- page 547 -->
The BR UA 4-H Fab Lab is the result of a partnership between the Navajo County Cooperative Extension 
of the University of Arizona and the Blue Ridge Unified School District. The facility is a fabrication labora-
tory, or Fab Lab, located in Pinetop-Lakeside. Functioning as an “open door open source educational         
resource”, it includes several classrooms designed for STEAM (Science, Technology, Engineering, Art and 
Math) work.  
This Lab is the first and only public school fab lab in Arizona and one of the most comprehensive school 
based makerspaces in the state.  It is a 7,500+ square foot facility offering a large selection of digital     
fabrication equipment and machines including: Fifteen 3D printers, 2 laser engravers, electronic and    
soldering work stations, vinyl printers and much more. 
FABRICATION LAB  
YOUTH DEVELOPMENT 
The Lab is being used for normally  
scheduled classes, including Digital  
Fabrication and Conceptual Physics 
and Engineering. Afterschool programs 
and clubs offered as part of the 4-H STEM  
programs for HS students include:  
 Physics and Engineering Club  
 NASA Rover team 
 Solar Go Kart Team  
 CubeSat team 
 JH 4-H physics and Engineering Club  
 Samsung Solve for Tomorrow STEM 
team.   
 Rocket Science and all things RC (students learn 
aeronautical engineering concepts by designing, 
building and flying scratch build RC aircraft, 
 Digital Photography/Printing  
BR UA 4-H Fab Lab Impacts: 
 2021 SARSEF Race the Sun, "Innovation" Award Winner and Second Place in the 
"Solar Efficiency" challenge for the Maker Class. 
 2021 Blue Ridge JH 4-H Physics and Engineering Club earned a Honorable Men-
tion, top 100 in the 2021 Samsung Solve for Tomorrow Challenge.

Board of Supervisors Regular 9. a. 1. a.        
Meeting Date: 02/08/2022  
Title: Tomhave Contract 
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Contract signed by County Manager/Assistant County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors
Resolution: Amendment Number 5, Contract for Professional Services with the Tomhave Group
Inc., to "Advocate for the Little Colorado at Winslow Levee, advance progress of surface
transportation on American Indian reservations located in Navajo County, and advance public
health policies of residents at disproportionate risk of contracting COVID-19 and other
life-threatening diseases”
Background:
This is the final allowable amendment to the professional services contract with the Tomhave
Group advocating for the Little Colorado at Winslow Levee Project.  It is a not to exceed cost of
$48,000, with approximately 80% of their contract work directed to negotiating a reduction in our
non-federal share of future design and construction project costs.  Given their previous work with
the Navajo Nation on tribal transportation and health issues, the contract anticipates a small
amount of time devoted to those issues, about 10% of contract time at most to each. Any time
spent on transportation and/or health issues will be charged to HURF or the General Fund,
respectively.

AMENDMENT NO. 5
CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES
In accordance with  the provisions of the above-referenced contract, the terms and
conditions are hereby amended as follows:
1. Term  and  Renewal:  T he  above  contract  is  hereby  mutually  extended  an
additional one-year term, February 28, 2022 through February 28, 2023 unless
terminated, canceled or extended as otherwise provided in the contract.  This is
the final allowable renewal for the above contract.
2. Payment:  The contract price is hereby amended for the additional sum of not-to-
exceed  forty-eight  thousand  ($48,000.00),  for  a  total  contract  price  of  one
hundred and fifty thousand dollars ($150,000).  
3. Scope: the scope is hereby amended to include the following:
 Increase monthly retainer from $3,000 to $4,000 per month.
 Continue advocating on behalf of Navajo County for the Winslow Levee 
Project specifically, but not limited to, negotiating a reduction to the local cost 
share and to the overall project cost. (80%).
 Advance progress of surface transportation on American Indian reservations 
located within Navajo County, including but not limited to, negotiating with 
federal and tribal decision makers on high priority tribal road projects (i.e. H- 
60), tribal roads bills (i.e. the Tribal Transportation Equity Act), and tribal 
infrastructure bills (i.e. HR 6141 to amend the Navajo-Hopi Settlement Act) 
(10%).
 Advance public health policies needed to protect county residents who are at 
disproportionate risk of contracting COVID-19 and other life-threatening 
diseases (10%)

and reaffirmed.

Board of Supervisors Regular 9. b. 1.        
Meeting Date: 02/08/2022  
Title: Burton RV and Boat Storage Facility District IV
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Berrin Nejad
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and adoption of Resolution 02-2022 approving/denying a special use permit that
allows construction of an RV and boat storage facility on 4.64 acres, located north of State Route
260 and west of Burton Road, in the Linden area. APN# 409-22-007S, T10N, R21E, S06 of the
GSRM.
Background:
Owner: Judy Stephenson Agent: Ryan Mell.
The applicant, Ryan Mell, is requesting approval of a Special Use Permit for an RV and Boat
Storage facility on a 4.6 acre property in Linden. The property is on Hwy 260 and zoned
commercial, but neighboring residential properties in the north.
 
The access to the site will be off Burton Road and the eastern portion of the property will be
dedicated to water retention.  The project will have 146 spaces with some having canopies.  A
solid fence will be placed around the property for screening from the neighbors.  The facility will
be self-service with an automated gate and light for the users and an illuminated sign will be
placed southwest corner of the lot, visible from Hwy 260.
The Planning and Zoning Commission heard this item in their November 18, 2021 public meeting
and after hearing presentations and neighbor comments, made a motion to recommend approval of
the Special Use Permit to the BOS with a 4-1 (Murph) vote.  
Recommendation: 
The Planning and Zoning Commission and staff recommend approval of this project and a Special
Use Permit request.

TO: Planning and Zoning Commission 
 
FROM: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director 
  
HEARING DATE: November 18, 2021   
 
PROJECT NUMBER:    SUP 21-013 BURTON RV STORAGE, DISTRICT IV:  A request for a 
Special Use Permit to construct an RV and boat storage facility with 
146 canopy covered spaces. The property is 4.64-acres in size, located 
north of State Route 260 and west of Burton Road, in the Linden area. 
APN# 409-22-007S, T10N, R21E,  S06 of the GSRM.  Owner: Judy 
Stephenson Agent: Ryan Mell. 
 
 
BACKGROUND and ANALYSIS: 
 
The applicant, Ryan Mell , is requesting an approval of Special Use Permit for an RV and Boat 
Storage facility on a 4.6 acres property in Linden. The property is on Hwy 260, commercial zone, 
but Burton Road leads to residential properties on the north.  
 
The access to the site will be off Burton Road and the eastern portion of the property will be 
dedicated to water retention.  The project will have 146 spaces with some having canopies.  A solid 
fence will be place around the property for screening from the neighbors.  The facility will be self-
service with an automated gate and light for the user and an illuminated sign will be placed southwest 
corner of the lot.

SITE LOCATION: 
  
 
 
 
Fence: A 6-foot decorative concrete wall will be places all around the project.  
 
Business Hours: Self-service, 24-hours.  
 
Signage:  Will be placed on southwest corner of the property.  
  
APPLICABLE CODES: 
 
Zoning Ordinance: Navajo County, Zoning Ordinance addresses Manufactured Home Parks in the 
following areas:  
 
ARTICLE 15 – (C-R) COMMERCIAL-RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT 
Section 1501 – Purpose  
The principle  purpose of this zoning district is to provide for shops and services in convenient 
locations to meet the daily needs of families in the immediate residential neighborhoods and 
wholesaling or distribution activities in locations where there is adequate access to major streets or 
highways.  Principal uses permitted in this zoning district include retail and wholesale commerce, 
commercial entertainment, and some uses of a light manufacturing nature.

ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
 
Section 2001 – Special Uses 
32. Storage of manufactured homes, travel trailers, boats and aircraft. 
 
34. Warehousing and storage, mini storage , including outdoor storage, i.e., recreational and 
equipment storage, public utilities, but not including the warehousing and storage of explosives or 
junk. 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised 
Statutes and Article 29.  
Notice of this item was published in the November 3, 2021 The Tribune.   Physical postings 
were made on the property on November 2, 2021 and postcards were mailed to all neighbors 
within 300 feet. 
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 This proposal meets policies and goals as laid out by Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan. 
 
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which 
the property is located. 
This property is zoned C-R Commercial- Residential and a storage facility is already existing 
and in use on the property.  A storage facility is compatible with permissible uses in this zoning 
district and surrounding uses.  
 
4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use. 
A storage facility would be compatible in the area.   
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
With the proper screening, meeting the vision triangle rules, there should be litt le to no issues 
for health, safety, or welfare for nearby properties.

COUNTY ATTORNEY:   
Deputy County Attorney has reviewed the proposed Site Plan  and supports Staff’s 
recommendations.                    Initial: B.S.C.

FIRE DEPARTMENT:

STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
Should the Planning and Zoning Commission recommend approval of the Special Use Permit to the 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors; Staff recommends the following conditions: 
1. The applicant shall obtain any required Navajo County permits, including, but not limited to, 
building, sewer connection, grading, drainage, and sign permits. 
2. Drainage and grading plans for all new site facilities shall be completed and approved by 
Navajo County Public Works prior to the issuance of a building permit for any of the new 
structures.  A storm water pollution preven tion plan shall be prepared, approved by the 
County and installed within 30 days of signature of this resolution.  The mitigation measures 
shall be maintained until the development of the parcel has completed.  
3. All the corners will be according to vision t riangle rules and regulations. No building or 
fencing shall obstruct the view of the traffic.  
4. No building or structure may be occupied prior to complete compliance with all appropriate 
Building Department requirements. 
5. All lighting on the property shall shielded to reflect downward, be motion-activated, and be 
in conformance with the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance. 
6. Hours of operation will be 24-hours self-service, 7 days a week. 
7. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site plan.  Any 
expansion or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to this Permit. 
8. This Special Use Permit shall run with the land pending compliance with all County, State, 
and Federal requirements.

P & Z COMMISSION HEARING 
 
11/18/2021 
 
ATTENDANCE 
P & Z Commissioners 
 
 
 
 
ATTENDED 
1. Wendell DeCross 
2. Randy Murph 
3. Chuck Howe (Zoom) 
4. Ruth Ann Smith 
5. Taber Heisler 
6.  George John (Joined through 
Zoom after Item #5) 
 
ABSENT 
1. Nick McVicker  
 
 STAFF ATTENDANCE 
1. John Osgood 
2. Berrin Nejad 
3. Cody Cooper 
4. Kristyn Saunders 
 
 
Meeting held at the Navajo County Board of Supervisors Chambers, Holbrook, Arizona 
and through Zoom.us – Time: 6:00 PM to 7:07 PM 
 
Chairman Howe called the meeting of the Navajo County Planning & Zoning Commission 
to order and explained the meeting procedures to the public. Vice Chairman Smith then 
led the Pledge of Allegiance. 
 
ITEM #1 – PLEDGE OF ALLEGIANCE 
 
ITEM #2 – REVIEW OF AGENDA BY COMMISSIONERS 
 
ITEM #3 – CALL TO PUBLIC FOR ITEMS NOT ON THE AGENDA 
 
ITEM #4 – CONSIDERATION OF APPROVAL OF MINUTES FROM THE COMMISION 
HEARING ON SEPTEMBER 16th 2021  
 
Chairman Howe called for a motion.  Commissioner Smith made a motion to approve 
the minutes. Commissioner Heisler seconded the motion.  Motion carried, 4 - 0. 
 
ITEM #5 –  SUP 21 -012 PINE ROSE MANUFACTURED HOME AND RV PARK, 
DISTRICT IV:   
A request for a Special Use Permit to build a 17-space manufactured home and RV park, 
including 14 manufactured home spaces and 3 RV spaces as well as a fourplex.  The

<!-- page 582 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
property is 2.83 -acres in size, located to  the east of Porter Mountain Road, between 
Amanda Drive and Peterson Road, in the Lakeside area.  APN# 212-26-082N, T09N, 
R22E, S24 of the GSRM. Owner: Juan Rosales. Agent:  Juan Rosales.  Table to 
December 16th meeting. 
 
Chairman Howe called for a motion.  Commissioner DeCross made a motion to 
approve the Table. Commissioner Heisler seconded the motion.  Motion carried, 4 - 0. 
 
ITEM #6 – SUP 21-013 BURTON RV STORAGE, DISTRICT IV: A request for a Special 
Use Permit to construct an RV and boat storage facility with 146 canopy covered spaces. 
The property is 4.64- acres in size, located north of State Route 260 and west of Burton 
Road, in the Linden area. APN# 409-22-007S, T10N, R21E, S06 of the GSRM.  Owner: 
Judy Stephenson Agent: Ryan Mell. 
 
Mrs. Nejad presented the item to the commission. The applicant, Ryan Mell, is requesting 
an approval for a Special Use Permit for an RV and Boat Storage facility on 4.6 acres of 
property in Linden along the Highway  260, and Burton Road. She continued saying that 
the use was allowed with commercial zoning for the property, and that Burton Road leads 
to residential areas northward; Access to the site will be from Burton Road and the eastern 
portion of the property will be dedicated to water retention. The project will have 146 
storage spaces with plans for canopies; a solid fence will be place around the property for 
screening from the neighbors, and the facility will be self-service with an automated gate 
and light for the users. Finally, an illuminated sign will be placed on the southwest corner 
of the lot  to denote the facility.  Staff has received calls and e -mails inquiring about the 
project, and the Fire Department sent staff a letter after the staff report had been prepared, 
noting that they approved of the project.  
 
The Chairman then opened for questions from the commission, and Commissioner Heisler 
noted that the staff report had the incorrect photo of the site, Berrin apologized and noted 
that she had a correct photo in the presentation. Commissioner DeCross asked about the 
ownership of the property, wanting to know about the name of the trust and Mr. Cooper  
noted that it was the Judy Lee Stephenson Trust LLC that held ownership of the property. 
 
The Chairman called to see if the applicant wanted to speak, and applicant Ryan Mell 
introduced himself and spoke to the commission about the project ; He  said that the 
neighbors’ concerns have been taken into consideration, especially their concerns about 
screening and vehicles getting backed up turning into the property - they have provided 
enough turning space to help alleviate that issue. Furthermore, after meeting with them a 
few days before the current hearing, they stated that they would like to put in an eight-foot 
decorative wall on the north side of the property. The lighting will only come on while the 
property is in use, to keep the lighting at a minimum, it will have 24-hour access and there 
will be a privacy screen that will prevent the light from disturbing the neighbors with dark-
skies compliant shielding. They will be using asphalt or crushed basalt, the fences will be 
solid save for the wrought-iron fence, there will be some minor landscaping and landscape 
lighting along the road fronts, with 3-to-6-inch rip rap. There will be canopies, but they will 
be added to the spaces depending on market appreciation of them.   
 
The Chairman asked if the commission had questions  for the applicant , Commissioner 
DeCross asked who the applicant was in relation to the owner, as he wanted to clarify just 
what was happening with the ownership and the application.

<!-- page 583 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
Applicant Ryan Mell said that he was the representative of the Blockheads LLC that was 
looking into purchasing the property, he further explained that they had submitted with the 
owner as Judy Stephenson as their contract for sale had not yet closed but would shortly. 
 
Commissioner DeCross asked about the wall on page 2, and the applicant explained what 
was intended, noting that Mrs. Nejad’s presentation was a sample from the company they 
were planning on using. 
 
Mrs. Nejad asked the applicant about his sudden change to an eight-foot fence, asking if 
that came up because of the most recent meeting with the neighbors; She further asked 
Mr. Cooper what the code allowed, and he explained that seven feet was the maximum 
height allowed by the current code. Mr. Mell said he would investigate the specifications 
of the fencing. 
 
Chairman Howe asked if there was any indication that an eight -foot fence would garner 
approval from the neighboring property owners, to which the audience spoke up to the 
commission that they were in the audience and would be willing to speak  to the 
Commission about this. 
 
Jarrell Abney said that he was there not really to speak toward the project but did point 
out that they had been in touch with the applicants and were concerned about the seven-
foot height limit and asked if there was any way to increase to eight feet.  
 
Commissioner DeCross asked about the staff report, noting that the applicant had 
submitted plans for what seemed to all be six-foot fencing. 
 
Mr. Abney said that the applicant had originally presented the item to them as such, but 
that after some recent discussion with them, the applicants were willing to give them an 
eight-foot-tall fence at their request.  
 
Commissioner Murph asked the speaker what concerns about six feet made eight feet 
more desirable. 
 
Mr. Abney said that as there would be Recreational Vehicles, it would greatly impact their 
view, likening it to a parking lot. The additional two feet would make the view less cluttered 
and provide more privacy. 
 
Commissioner Murph spoke favorably of the idea, and the applicant’s engineer 
Curtis asked if eight feet was allowed in Residential  zonings, to which Mr. Cooper  said 
that no, they would not be.  
  
There was some discussion about the logistics of the code, as well as comments from 
Public Works Director John Osgood to the commission stating that there was a significant 
difference with engineering a six-foot fence versus  an eight-foot fence, the building 
departments’ chief inspector had interpreted the recently adopted 2015 International  
Building Code that clearly stated the allowance of a seven-foot-tall fence but did not allow 
for eight feet. He did state that they would review the issue and see if something could be 
done to allow for an eight-foot fence. 
 
Mr. Mell said that they want to try and please their neighbors so they would be happy to 
work with staff to find a solution for an eight-foot fence.

<!-- page 584 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 
Chairman Howe asked for any more in favor, then if there were any opposed, and 
Stephanie Marshall approached to speak and said that they would be right there on the 
corner of the proposed entrance and was concerned about property value loss and said 
that they wanted to heavily encourage an eight-foot fence because that would be less of 
an impact with the sight and sound of the RV storage. 
 
JoAnn Abney also spoke to the commission, stating that they had just purchased this 
property, and they were not aware o f the zoning when they made the purchase, noting 
that while she was not in favor of the project, she too would prefer an eight-foot fence. 
 
Mrs. Nejad commented that the eight-foot fence is not an issue that staff can handle nor 
is the proposed item acceptable for a variance , as it did not deal with a hardship of the 
land. 
 
Commissioner Howe spoke to the applicant, noting that they could withdraw and resubmit, 
or they can continue as submitted. 
 
Mr. Mell said they’d do their best to please the neighbors, and said that if they needed to, 
they would withdraw to try and find a solution to the fence. 
 
To this, Mr. Abney spoke up and noted that they would be willing to work with the applicant 
as submitted and did not wish to stand in the  way of their application.  He further noted 
that they do appreciate the efforts made to work with them on this matter.   
 
Commissioner DeCross stated the role of the Commission was solely to give a 
recommendation to the Board of Supervisors, and that it was not up to them to grant or 
deny anything, and further said that their role was not to get into details for the wall height, 
that is a role for the building department. 
 
Note: Commissioner George John joined in time to hear this project and voted. 
 
Chairman Howe called for a motion.  Commissioner Decross made a motion to 
recommend approval of the Special Use Permit. Commissioner Smith seconded the 
motion.  Motion carried, 4 – 1 (Murph) 
 
ITEM #7 – REVIEW AND APPROVAL OF 2022 CALENDAR: A request from staff to review 
and approve the 2022 meeting calendar. 
 
Chairman Howe called for a motion.  Commissioner DeCross made a motion to 
approve the calendar. Commissioner Murph seconded the motion. Motion carried, 5 - 0 
 
 
ITEM # 8 – CONFIRMATION OF DECEMBER 2021 MEETING :  A request from staff to 
confirm the date of the December 16th meeting to ensure there are no conflicts and that there 
will be a quorum.   
 
The Commission all confirmed attendance, either in person or through Zoom. 
 
ITEM #9 – REPORT FROM STAFF TO THE COMMISSION:

The meeting was adjourned at 7:07.

RESOLUTION ____-22 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS APPROVING 
A SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR BURTON RV AND BOAT STORAGE FACILITY WITH 
146 SPACES. 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit  was duly filed on  September 30, 
2021 by Ryan Mell to allow construction of a storage facility; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County:  
APN# 409-22-007S, T10N, R21E, S06 of the GSRM; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on November 18, 2021; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Superv isors after considering the testimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, fou nd that 
the Special Use Permit is consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and should 
be approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Superviso rs hereby 
approves the Special Use Permit, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. The applicant shall obtain any required Navajo County permits, including, but not limited 
to, building, sewer connection, grading, drainage, and sign permits. 
 
2. Drainage and grading plans for all new site facilit ies shall be completed and approved by 
Navajo County Public Works prior to the issuance of a building permit for any of the new 
structures.  A storm water pollution prevention plan shall be prepared, appro ved by the 
County, and installed within 30 days of signature of this resolution.  The mitigation 
measures shall be maintained until the development of the parcel has completed.  
 
3. All the corners will be according to vision triangle rules and regulations. No building or 
fencing shall obstruct the view of the traffic.  
 
4. No building or structure may be occupied prior to complete compliance with all 
appropriate Building Department requirements. 
 
5. All lighting on the property shall shielded to reflect downward, be mo tion-activated, and 
be in conformance with the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance.

<!-- page 587 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
6. Hours of operation will be 24-hours self-service, 7 days a week. 
 
7. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site plan.  
Any expansion or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to this Permit. 
 
8. This Special Use Permit shall run with the land pending compliance with all County, 
State, and Federal requirements.
